<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.
</collaboration_mode>

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

## assistant

For **2026-09-21 through 2026-09-25 so far**, the calendar shows **14 completed, confirmed bookings**. Four have an attached “Notes by Gemini” document. The other **10 have no Calendar attachment**, and I found no matching Google Doc created this week. That identifies gaps to investigate; it does **not** establish that all 10 meetings were attended or that their transcripts are missing everywhere.

| Date | Time (Toronto) | Event | Notes attached |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Sep 21 | 13:45 | Asia | No |
| Sep 21 | 15:20 | Sofia | No |
| Sep 21 | 16:40 | Sandra | No |
| Sep 21 | 18:30 | Celia | Yes |
| Sep 22 | 16:00 | Charles | No |
| Sep 22 | 18:10 | Ore | No |
| Sep 22 | 20:00 | Amanda | Yes |
| Sep 23 | 15:30 | Sofia | Yes |
| Sep 23 | 17:00 | Sandra | Yes |
| Sep 23 | 18:30 | Yahunathan | No |
| Sep 24 | 13:00 | Consultation with Angie Pasquel | No |
| Sep 24 | 14:30 | Ivory | No |
| Sep 24 | 17:00 | Venusa | No |
| Sep 24 | 18:20 | Sandra | No |

Four more confirmed sessions are scheduled for Sep 25–26: Charles, Zeina, Sofia, and Nicole. Four cancelled calendar entries are excluded. I also noticed that the document attached to Sofia’s Sep 23 event has **18:33 EDT** in its title, despite the event starting at **15:30**; that match needs checking.

❓ **Q1 - What counts as a gap?** Should every *attended* tutoring session and consultation have a transcript attached to its Calendar event, or is a transcript stored elsewhere sufficient?

➡️ **My recommendation:** Count a meeting as covered when its transcript can be matched reliably anywhere, but flag a missing Calendar attachment separately.

---

❓ **Q2 - What should we do after identifying a gap?** Once we verify which of these bookings took place, should I find existing transcripts and attach them, or stop at a report of what is missing?

➡️ **My recommendation:** Find and match existing transcripts first, then show you the verified gaps before changing Calendar events.

Read that folder\'s `CLAUDE.md` before touching anything in it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The primary/mirror boundary (D4 — read before regenerating anything)\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery file in a student folder is one of two kinds, and the distinction decides what a rebuild may overwrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Primary — the brain is the only home.** Transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, study plans, anything Joshua or an agent authored about the student. **Never regenerate, never overwrite; retain forever.**\\r\\n- **Mirror — a projection of a live backend.** Session/booking state (Cal.com + Google Calendar), homework/dossier/publish state (LSAT U Supabase), payment events (Stripe), correspondence indexes (Gmail). On any disagreement the backend wins, silently.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Convention:** mirror files MUST be named `*.generated.md` (or carry `generated: true` frontmatter). A file without that marker is primary by definition and must be treated as precious. Pre-2026-07-16 files (`email-context.md`, `payment-links.md`, `transcripts.md`) predate the marker — treat as primary until individually re-derived and renamed. No Markdown file here is ever the source of operational truth — no package balances, no running totals (all services are paid upfront; Cal.com answers sessions-used).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Standard files per student folder\\r\\n\\r\\n- `<student-slug>.md` — compact student index and routing page (primary).\\r\\n- `email-context.md` — synthesized Gmail context.\\r\\n- `email-correspondence.md` — Gmail message-id corpus for the student\'s confirmed address.\\r\\n- `payment-links.md` — verified payment, billing, and scheduling links.\\r\\n- `transcripts.md` — transcript and meeting-note inventory.\\r\\n- `teaching-notes.md` — durable, evidence-backed learning judgments and teaching patterns (primary).\\r\\n- `open-loops.md` — unfinished follow-ups and pending decisions (primary).\\r\\n- `live-status.generated.md` — refreshable operational summary from Calendar, Gmail, payments, and LSAT U (generated mirror).\\r\\n- `transcript-downloads/` — full local transcript exports (primary — the brain is their only home).\\r\\n- `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` — new self-contained session packages.\\r\\n- `assignment-log.md` — running log of assigned work and notification evidence so repeats are avoided (primary).\\r\\n- `Practice_Test_Reports/` — the student\'s PT score reports as received (intake form, screenshots, exports). Raw evidence — read for diagnosis, never edit.\\r\\n- `Student_Specific_Homework/` — work assigned to this student specifically. Distinct from the shared banks in `corpus/Curriculum/*/Homework/`, which are the source material it draws from.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- New student → new folder `students/<slug>/` with a `<slug>.md` profile page.\\r\\n- New session → `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` containing `transcript.md`, `summary.md`, and `topic-map.md`.\\r\\n- `transcript.md` preserves the raw source. `summary.md` records the auto-saved summary, action items, and appended corrections. `topic-map.md` is a timestamped link index to relevant LSAT concepts, student records, and source material.\\r\\n- Session summaries are append-only. Later corrections go in a dated correction section and never erase the original summary.\\r\\n- Generated operational state is clearly marked with `generated: true` and never overrides primary teaching records.\\r\\n- Wikilink student pages into corpus concepts and LSAT U material as they become relevant.\\r\\n- Tier A note: transcripts and correspondence are trackable (Tier-A amendment, 2026-07-16). True secrets (credentials, IDs, payment instruments) still never enter Git — `private/` only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Operating contract\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each new consultation or transcript, Jay 2 must report what was found, what was created or updated, what was inferred, what remains missing, what is recommended, and what Joshua should confirm or reject. Use for \\"post-session note\\", \\"recap note\\", \\"write the recap\\", \\"take a recap for a student\\", weekly recap intake, or after a transcript is ingested. Drafts by default; post or assign only when Joshua explicitly asks.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Session recap\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter every session Joshua wants a durable set of lecture notes. The student should be\\r\\nable to return several days later, understand the lesson without remembering a specific\\r\\nquestion, and apply the method to new work. It is teaching content, not an email or a\\r\\nsession summary.\\r\\n\\r\\nRead `skills/reference/session-recap-quality-reference.md` before drafting. Its Sofia\\r\\nnote is the quality bar for depth, hierarchy, nuance, and practical usefulness. Copy the\\r\\nmethod, not the conditional-logic headings.\\r\\n\\r\\nDraft and file the note by default. If Joshua explicitly says to post, assign, publish,\\r\\nor send it to the student, complete that as a separate LSAT U platform action after the\\r\\ndraft is ready and verify what landed. Never infer posting permission from a request to\\r\\nwrite a note.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Get the transcript on disk first\\r\\n\\r\\nTranscripts come from Google Meet\'s Gemini notes. The calendar event carries the Drive\\r\\ndoc as an attachment; some sessions have the doc in Drive but never got it attached.\\r\\nCheck both.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n# events in the window (guest email is the join key, never the event title)\\r\\ngws calendar events list --params \'{\\"calendarId\\":\\"primary\\",\\"timeMin\\":\\"YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00Z\\",\\"timeMax\\":\\"YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00Z\\",\\"singleEvents\\":true,\\"orderBy\\":\\"startTime\\",\\"maxResults\\":100}\' --format json\\r\\n\\r\\n# docs Gemini wrote in the window, attached or not (\\"Meeting started ...\\" = untitled event)\\r\\ngws drive files list --params \'{\\"q\\":\\"name contains \'\\"\'\\"\'Notes by Gemini\'\\"\'\\"\' and modifiedTime > \'\\"\'\\"\'YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00\'\\"\'\\"\'\\",\\"fields\\":\\"files(id,name,createdTime)\\",\\"orderBy\\":\\"createdTime desc\\",\\"pageSize\\":50}\' --format json\\r\\n\\r\\n# export as plain text (NOT text/markdown: that build is 10x larger and escapes everything)\\r\\ngws drive files export --params \'{\\"fileId\\":\\"<id>\\",\\"mimeType\\":\\"text/plain\\"}\' -o <scratch>/<name>.txt\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Drive result can include Markdown copies uploaded by an earlier intake. Count native\\r\\nGoogle Docs as source transcripts and use the file ID to deduplicate. Do not count an\\r\\nuploaded `YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>-notes-by-gemini.md` copy as another session. Reconcile the\\r\\nfull week against Calendar before exporting so unattached docs and events with no doc\\r\\nboth remain visible in the report.\\r\\n\\r\\nFile to `students/<slug>/transcript-downloads/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>-notes-by-gemini.md`.\\r\\nResolve the person with `students/_aliases.md`; for a \\"Meeting started\\" doc, count\\r\\nspeaker labels in the transcript (`grep -o -E \\"^[A-Z][a-z]+( [A-Z][a-z]+)?:\\" | sort | uniq -c`).\\r\\nConsultations and people without a folder go to `students/_other-sessions/`.\\r\\n**Never create a student folder without asking.** Skip anything already on disk.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Google Classroom calendars (`c_classroom...`) have been empty since 2026; the\\r\\nprimary calendar holds every session.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Read the whole transcript, not the Gemini summary\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Gemini \\"Quick notes\\" flatten what he said. Read the transcript section. It is 40\\r\\nto 90 KB; that is the cost of a recap that is true.\\r\\n\\r\\nExtract:\\r\\n\\r\\n- the governing concept and distinctions Joshua taught;\\r\\n- the student\'s actual confusion or decision point;\\r\\n- the reasoning procedure, including why each step works;\\r\\n- legal and illegal moves, edge cases, diagnostic checks, and shortcuts;\\r\\n- examples that can stand alone outside the original question; and\\r\\n- the exact practice and follow-ups that were assigned.\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not write a play-by-play of answer choices. A detail from a specific question belongs\\r\\nonly when it demonstrates a reusable move. If the explanation needs more support, search\\r\\nJoshua\'s corpus first. Use an authoritative outside source only when Joshua points to it\\r\\nor the corpus cannot resolve the concept. Keep source research outside the student note.\\r\\nThe session transcript remains the authority for what happened and what was assigned.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Write the recap\\r\\n\\r\\nPath: `students/<slug>/recaps/YYYY-MM-DD-recap.md`. One file per session. Primary\\r\\nrecord: never regenerate over an existing one; a redo gets a dated correction section.\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\n---\\r\\ntitle: Recap · <Student> · YYYY-MM-DD\\r\\ntype: recap\\r\\nrecord_kind: primary\\r\\nstudent: <slug>\\r\\nsession_date: YYYY-MM-DD\\r\\nsource: transcript-downloads/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>-notes-by-gemini.md\\r\\nstatus: draft\\r\\nposted: false\\r\\nposted_at:\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# <Concept the student needs>\\r\\n\\r\\n<Open directly with the governing distinction or decision. One short orientation\\r\\nparagraph. No greeting, sign-off, praise, generic scene-setting, \\"Today...\\", or recap of\\r\\nwhich answers were correct.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## <A descriptive header that teaches the first part of the method>\\r\\n\\r\\n<Define the idea before applying it. Build a reusable reference with the combination of\\r\\nshort explanation, numbered steps, a compact table, or a spaced example that best fits\\r\\nthe material.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## <The next conceptual move>\\r\\n\\r\\n<Explain why it works, how to recognize when to use it, what does not follow, and how\\r\\nthe student can check the move. Add boundary cases or common traps when they mattered in\\r\\nthe session.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## <A repeatable process>\\r\\n\\r\\n1. <A decision the student can actually make.>\\r\\n2. <The next observable check.>\\r\\n3. <How to verify the inference or reject the trap.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Before next session\\r\\n\\r\\n- [ ] <What was actually assigned, as the student would recognise it.>\\r\\n- [ ] <Only what the transcript supports. No invented drills.>\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes for me (not posted)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Weak spots:** <tags>\\r\\n- **Follow-ups on my side:** <things Joshua promised: send materials, assign a drill, schedule>\\r\\n- **Watch:** <a teaching judgment worth carrying into teaching-notes.md if it repeats>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything above the `---` rule is the student note and must stand alone when pasted.\\r\\nEverything below is admin-only.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe headings above are scaffolding, not a mandatory outline. Organize around the lesson\'s\\r\\nlogic. Use enough depth to teach the method fully, but remove any section that does not\\r\\nhelp the student use it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice rules (from `wiki/teaching-voice.md`, Coaching register)\\r\\n\\r\\n- A post-session note is a set of lecture notes, not an email. Use a concept title,\\r\\n  descriptive headers, numbered procedures, and spaced examples. Do not use a greeting\\r\\n  or sign-off.\\r\\n- Short sentences. One idea per line. Air between ideas.\\r\\n- Write for a student returning days later. Do not rely on \\"remember that question\\" or\\r\\n  unexplained references to a person, scenario, or answer choice.\\r\\n- Teach the reasoning; never just state the answer. \\"A reverses the timeline: the\\r\\n  symptom stops, then the boss assumes the cause was fixed\\" beats \\"A was wrong\\".\\r\\n- Praise only when it names a durable skill or meaningful change and ties to their\\r\\n  history. Never praise the mere fact that an answer was correct.\\r\\n- Honest about the test, never blunt about the student. When it slipped, say it is a\\r\\n  normal place to slip and move to the fix.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Periods, colons, the middot (·).\\r\\n- No \\"unlock\\", \\"dive deep\\", \\"elevate\\", \\"game-changer\\", \\"seamless\\", \\"robust\\", no emoji.\\r\\n- No internal vocabulary: no \\"beat\\", no \\"door\\", no tool or model names, no \\"transcript\\"\\r\\n  or \\"Gemini\\" in the student note.\\r\\n- Only what the transcript supports. If he did not assign it, it is not in \\"Before\\r\\n  next session\\".\\r\\n- Read `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` before writing; every past wording fix is there.\\r\\n- For conditional LSAT notes, distinguish an asserted fact from a general rule. A\\r\\n  rule states what follows if its trigger is present; it does not establish that the\\r\\n  trigger occurred. Some stimuli contain rules and no facts. Never invent a fact to\\r\\n  trigger a rule.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Validate, log, and hand back\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore handoff, check that:\\r\\n\\r\\n- the opening begins with content rather than commentary about the session;\\r\\n- the note explains both the move and the reason it is valid;\\r\\n- examples make sense without the original question;\\r\\n- hierarchy is visible at a glance;\\r\\n- practice matches the transcript; and\\r\\n- the student-facing portion contains no private context, transcript references, or\\r\\n  administrative follow-ups.\\r\\n\\r\\n- Append the session line to the student\'s `transcripts.md` if one exists (primary,\\r\\n  append-only), and any promise he made to `open-loops.md`.\\r\\n- Append one line to `wiki/log.md`: `## YYYY-MM-DD recap | <students>` with paths.\\r\\n- Report: which recaps were written, which sessions had no transcript (list them, with\\r\\n  the reason if known), and anything that needs his call (a new name, a folder to\\r\\n  create). If Joshua explicitly requested a platform action, report its verification\\r\\n  separately from the draft.\\r\\n"}}}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"i":2,"result":{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"c78f70","wall_time_seconds":1.7644134,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":10,"output":".agents/skills\\\\session-recap\\\\SKILL.md\\n"}}}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\r\nname: ingest\r\ndescription: >\r\n  Front door for getting anything into the brain. Use whenever Joshua says\r\n  "ingest this", "ingest the latest clips", "process the transcripts", "pull\r\n  this week\'s transcripts", "file these notes", "I saved an article", "what\'s\r\n  in the inbox", or drops a file, a screenshot, or a score report and expects\r\n  it filed — even when he does not say "ingest". Routes each input to its\r\n  pipeline (clips, Gemini session transcripts, Superwhisper recordings, student\r\n  evidence, bulk exports), dedupes by hash first, and never modifies a source.\r\nallowed-tools: Bash Read Write Edit Glob Grep\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Ingest\r\n\r\nOne front door. Joshua should never have to know which pipeline a thing belongs\r\nto, and no session should spend its first minutes hunting for a file he just\r\nsaved. The skill ends when the source is archived with provenance, the\r\ndistillation is filed where the next session will look, and `wiki/log.md`\r\nsays so. Anything short of that is a pile, not an inbox.\r\n\r\n## Route first\r\n\r\nIdentify what is being ingested, then jump to that section. If it is genuinely\r\nambiguous, ask once. Do not guess and file it somewhere wrong.\r\n\r\n| Input | Where it lands | Section |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| Web clip from Obsidian Web Clipper (`.md` in `raw/inbox/`) | `raw/inbox/` | [Clips](#clips) |\r\n| A clip that is a YouTube / video page | `raw/inbox/` | [Clips](#clips), read via `watch` |\r\n| Gemini "Notes by Gemini" from a live session (this week, last week) | Google Drive | [Transcripts, live](#transcripts-live) |\r\n| Archival Gemini `.docx` / `.txt` (Apr to Jul 2026 Takeout batch) | `raw/data-about-me/transcripts/` | [Transcripts, archival](#transcripts-archival) |\r\n| Superwhisper `recordings/` dirs (`meta.json` + `output.wav`) or `settings.json` | wherever they were dropped | hand to `superwhisper`; not this skill\'s |\r\n| Student evidence: PT score report, screenshot, homework export | `~/Downloads`, chat attachment | [Student evidence](#student-evidence) |\r\n| A source document meant to become wiki knowledge | `raw/` | dedupe below, then hand to `second-brain-ingest` |\r\n| Takeout or platform dump | `raw/data-about-me/` | [Bulk exports](#bulk-exports) |\r\n\r\n`second-brain-ingest` owns `raw/` to `wiki/` knowledge pages. `session-recap`\r\nowns writing the student-facing recap. This skill gets the source on disk,\r\narchived, and distilled; it does not duplicate either of them.\r\n\r\n## Always, before any pipeline\r\n\r\n1. **Find the file without asking where it is.** Search newest-first:\r\n   ```\r\n   raw/inbox/   raw/sources/   ~/Downloads/   ~/Desktop/   students/*/transcript-downloads/\r\n   ```\r\n   Ask only if that comes up empty. He has answered "where is it" too many\r\n   times. `raw/inbox/` is the single intake queue; the old top-level\r\n   `Clippings/` folder was folded into `raw/sources/clips/` on 2026-09-08, so a\r\n   `Clippings/` path in an old note is stale.\r\n2. **Dedupe by content hash, not by name.**\r\n   ```\r\n   sha256sum <candidate>\r\n   grep -rl "<hash>" raw/sources/*/*/SHA256SUMS\r\n   ```\r\n   A hit means it has been ingested; say so and stop. Byte-identical copies\r\n   under different names are how three copies of one clip piled up before\r\n   2026-09-08. Never delete the duplicate without asking.\r\n3. **Check the log.** `grep -n "<source name>" wiki/log.md`. Already there\r\n   means confirm with Joshua before re-running.\r\n4. **Never modify the source.** `raw/` is immutable. Sources are read and\r\n   archived, never edited in place, and never moved except by the archive step\r\n   of the pipeline that owns them.\r\n\r\n## Clips\r\n\r\nObsidian Web Clipper writes `.md` files to `raw/inbox/`. Anything else sitting\r\nthere (a `recordings/` tree, a `settings.json`) is not a clip; route it by the\r\ntable above and tell Joshua it was in the wrong place rather than processing it\r\nhere.\r\n\r\n1. List `raw/inbox/*.md` newest-first. "The latest" means the newest; a named\r\n   topic means match on it; several new ones means list them and ask which.\r\n2. Dedupe (above).\r\n3. Read the clip fully. If it is a video page with no transcript in the clip,\r\n   run `watch` on the URL to get one; the clip alone is a thumbnail.\r\n4. Summarize 3 to 5 takeaways and ask which to emphasize or skip. Wait.\r\n5. Write or update the wiki page(s) the clip touches. One authoritative page per\r\n   subject; grow the existing page rather than a parallel one.\r\n6. Update `wiki/index.md`.\r\n7. Append to `wiki/log.md`: `## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | <title>` with the source\r\n   path and the pages changed.\r\n8. **Archive.** Move the clip from `raw/inbox/` to `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/`\r\n   (kebab-cased title) with:\r\n   - `PROVENANCE.md`: source URL, author, published date, clipped date,\r\n     archived date, size, where the distilled output landed. Copy the shape from\r\n     any sibling folder.\r\n   - `SHA256SUMS`: `sha256sum <clip> PROVENANCE.md > SHA256SUMS`.\r\n\r\n   Done means `raw/inbox/` no longer contains the clip. A file still there\r\n   means "not yet processed" and nothing else.\r\n\r\nOne clip at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch.\r\n\r\n## Transcripts, live\r\n\r\nGemini writes "Notes by Gemini" docs to Google Drive after each Meet session.\r\nThe brain\'s copy is `students/<slug>/transcript-downloads/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>-notes-by-gemini.md`\r\n(primary record: the brain is its only home). Getting them there is step 1 of\r\n`session-recap`; follow that section exactly (Calendar sweep by guest email,\r\nDrive sweep for unattached docs, export as `text/plain`, resolve the person\r\nwith `students/_aliases.md`, consultations and unknowns to\r\n`students/_other-sessions/`). It is the single source of truth for the pull;\r\ndo not re-derive the `gws` commands here.\r\n\r\nThen, per transcript that is new on disk:\r\n\r\n1. Read the whole transcript, not the Gemini summary at the top. The summary\r\n   flattens what the student actually did; the distillation has to name the\r\n   answer they picked and the line they misread.\r\n2. Append to the student\'s `teaching-notes.md` (create it from a sibling if the\r\n   folder lacks one; it is a standard primary file): what was taught, where they\r\n   struggled, what was assigned, what to follow up. Dated heading, append-only,\r\n   never regenerate.\r\n3. Anything needing an action goes to `open-loops.md` (same rules).\r\n4. Add one line to `transcripts.md` so the folder\'s inventory matches disk.\r\n5. Never write to a `*.generated.md` file; those mirror a live backend.\r\n6. Append to `wiki/log.md`.\r\n\r\nDone means every session in the window either has its transcript on disk and\r\ndistilled, or is listed in the log as "no Gemini doc anywhere" with the date and\r\nstudent, so the next session does not sweep it again.\r\n\r\nIf Joshua wants the recap note as well, that is `session-recap`; say so rather\r\nthan writing one here.\r\n\r\n## Transcripts, archival\r\n\r\nThe Takeout batch of 52 `.docx` / `.txt` files in `raw/data-about-me/transcripts/`\r\n(2026-04-28 to 2026-07-15) was distilled on 2026-07-20 (`wiki/log.md`). It is\r\ngit-ignored PII; never read the folder wholesale into context.\r\n\r\n**Start with the inventory. Do not re-derive the mapping by hand.**\r\n\r\n```\r\npython system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py              # full report\r\npython system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --unmatched  # only the gaps\r\npython system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --json       # machine-readable\r\npython system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --text "<file>.docx"   # one transcript as text\r\n```\r\n\r\nIt maps each file to a folder using `students/_aliases.md`, knows that former\r\nstudents live in `students/non-students/`, and prints what it cannot resolve.\r\n`--text` exists because this box has no python-docx and no pandoc; it is how a\r\n`.docx` gets read. The script is read-only.\r\n\r\nFor an unmapped file, resolve before filing:\r\n- a name variant of an existing student: add it under `## Aliases` in\r\n  `students/_aliases.md`, re-run, continue;\r\n- a consultation or one-off: add under `## Prospects, no record yet` (the\r\n  section the script parses) so it stops surfacing;\r\n- a real recurring student with no folder: **ask Joshua before creating one.**\r\n  A folder is a judgment about the relationship, not a filing detail.\r\n\r\nThen distill exactly as in the live section, steps 1 to 6. The `.docx` stays in\r\n`raw/`; what lands in the student folder is what a human wants to read before\r\nthe next session.\r\n\r\nIf the inventory gets a mapping wrong, fix the script or the alias file. Working\r\naround it silently means the next session re-derives the same mapping.\r\n\r\n## Student evidence\r\n\r\nPT score reports, intake screenshots, homework exports, anything about one\r\nstudent that arrives as a file.\r\n\r\n1. Identify the student (filename, chat context, contents). Ask if unclear.\r\n2. Dedupe (above).\r\n3. Copy into the student folder under the matching standard subfolder from\r\n   `students/README.md`: score reports to `Practice_Test_Reports/`, assigned\r\n   work to `Student_Specific_Homework/`, otherwise the folder root with a dated\r\n   name. These are raw evidence: read for diagnosis, never edited.\r\n4. If it changes a judgment, append the judgment to `teaching-notes.md` with the\r\n   file as its cited source.\r\n5. Log it.\r\n\r\n## Bulk exports\r\n\r\nTakeout and platform dumps land in `raw/data-about-me/` and are git-ignored PII.\r\n\r\n- Never read one wholesale; it has blown the context window before.\r\n- Inventory first (file counts, types, date ranges), report, and let Joshua\r\n  pick what matters.\r\n- Duplicate exports are common and large. Report duplicates by hash and size;\r\n  do not delete without asking.\r\n- Analysis across many files goes to a sub-agent that returns a summary, not\r\n  file contents.\r\n\r\n## Hard rules\r\n\r\n- **Never delete or move a source** except the archive step of its own\r\n  pipeline. Flag duplicates; Joshua decides.\r\n- **Never create a student folder without asking.**\r\n- **Never write to `*.generated.md`.**\r\n- **Dedupe before ingesting, every time.**\r\n- **One source at a time, supervised,** unless Joshua explicitly says batch.\r\n- **Government IDs stay out.** If an export contains them, stop and say so.\r\n- Commands here are for this Windows box (Git Bash: `sha256sum`, `python`).\r\n  A note that says `shasum -a 256`, `python3`, or `/Users/joshuaf/...` is from\r\n  the Mac and is stale, not a different pipeline.\r\n'}]

## user

&#x20;It's not for students to open; it's for me to have. Do you know what the student folders are for?


## assistant

Yes. I misstated the audience. The student folders are your internal working records: full transcript evidence, teaching judgments, assignments, correspondence, and open loops, organized so you, Claude, and Codex can prepare for a session and act afterward. They are not pages for students to open. I’ll make the folder routers serve that internal workflow and keep student-facing recaps separate.

This is one of the brain's three knowledge systems, beside [[../wiki/index|wiki]] and [[../corpus/index|corpus]].\n+One folder per student, holding **everything** about them (D2, locked 2026-07-16): transcripts, correspondence, homework, notes, schedules, profile. These are internal working records for Joshua, Claude, and Codex, not pages for students to open. Open the student's folder and their whole working record is there. This is one of the brain's three knowledge systems, beside [[../wiki/index|wiki]] and [[../corpus/index|corpus]].\n@@\n-- `<student-slug>.md` — compact student index and routing page (primary).\n+- `<student-slug>.md` — internal entry page and folder router (primary). Its `## Open this record` block links only to local files that exist.\n@@\n-- `transcripts.md` — transcript and meeting-note inventory.\n+- `transcripts.md` — transcript and meeting-note inventory. Its `## Local session files` table links directly to every full local Markdown export and every session package, newest first. A Google Doc link is provenance, never the only way to read a session.\n@@\n-- Keep the original Gemini export in `transcript-downloads/` when available. Build the session package from verified session evidence, and link it from `transcripts.md`; older exports may predate the package convention.\n+- Keep the original full Gemini export as a local Markdown file in `transcript-downloads/` when available. Build the session package from verified session evidence, and link both the export and package from `transcripts.md`; older exports may predate the package convention.\n@@\n - Tier A note: transcripts and correspondence are trackable (Tier-A amendment, 2026-07-16). True secrets (credentials, IDs, payment instruments) still never enter Git — `private/` only.\n+\n+## Internal routing contract after each session\n+\n+1. Match the Calendar event and Gemini Doc by guest email, date, and content. Preserve the complete source export in `transcript-downloads/`; never rely on a Drive link as the record.\n+2. Create or update the session's `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` package without replacing a primary transcript or summary. Record any source mismatch as a dated correction.\n+3. Add a newest-first row to `transcripts.md` under `## Local session files`: date, direct local full-export link, local package links, and source/status. Include raw-only historical exports and packages even when the other half is absent.\n+4. Update evidence-backed `teaching-notes.md` and `open-loops.md`. Refresh the student's `<slug>.md` `## Open this record` block so Joshua, Claude, and Codex can reach the latest work in two clicks. Link only to files that exist.\n+5. Check that all local links resolve, that the full Markdown export contains the transcript rather than only Gemini's summary, and that the session index has no unlinked export or package. Leave attendance and missing-source uncertainty explicit.\n*** End Patch";
text(await tools.apply_patch(patch));

const patch = "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\system\\scripts\\check-student-records.py\n+\"\"\"Check the local navigation contract for current student folders.\n+\n+Read-only. Run from the repository root with:\n+    python system/scripts/check-student-records.py\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+from __future__ import annotations\n+\n+import re\n+import sys\n+from pathlib import Path\n+\n+\n+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\n+STUDENTS = ROOT / \"students\"\n+WIKILINK = re.compile(r\"\\[\\[([^\\]|]+)(?:\\|[^\\]]*)?\\]\\]\")\n+\n+\n+def section(markdown: str, heading: str) -> str | None:\n+    match = re.search(rf\"(?m)^## {re.escape(heading)}\\s*$\", markdown)\n+    if not match:\n+        return None\n+    end = re.search(r\"(?m)^## \", markdown[match.end() :])\n+    return markdown[match.end() : match.end() + end.start()] if end else markdown[match.end() :]\n+\n+\n+def linked_files(markdown: str, folder: Path) -> tuple[set[Path], list[str]]:\n+    paths: set[Path] = set()\n+    broken: list[str] = []\n+    for target in WIKILINK.findall(markdown):\n+        target = target.split(\"#\", 1)[0].strip()\n+        if not target:\n+            continue\n+        path = (ROOT / target) if target.startswith(\"students/\") else (folder / target)\n+        if not path.suffix:\n+            path = path.with_suffix(\".md\")\n+        path = path.resolve()\n+        paths.add(path)\n+        if not path.is_file():\n+            broken.append(target)\n+    return paths, broken\n+\n+\n+def main() -> int:\n+    errors: list[str] = []\n+    folders = [\n+        path\n+        for path in sorted(STUDENTS.iterdir())\n+        if path.is_dir() and not path.name.startswith(\"_\") and path.name != \"non-students\"\n+    ]\n+    exports = packages = 0\n+\n+    for folder in folders:\n+        profile = folder / f\"{folder.name}.md\"\n+        inventory = folder / \"transcripts.md\"\n+        if not profile.is_file() or not inventory.is_file():\n+            errors.append(f\"{folder.name}: missing profile or transcripts.md\")\n+            continue\n+\n+        profile_section = section(profile.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"), \"Open this record\")\n+        inventory_section = section(inventory.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"), \"Local session files\")\n+        if profile_section is None:\n+            errors.append(f\"{folder.name}: profile lacks 'Open this record'\")\n+            continue\n+        if inventory_section is None:\n+            errors.append(f\"{folder.name}: transcripts.md lacks 'Local session files'\")\n+            continue\n+\n+        profile_links, profile_broken = linked_files(profile_section, folder)\n+        inventory_links, inventory_broken = linked_files(inventory_section, folder)\n+        if inventory.resolve() not in profile_links:\n+            errors.append(f\"{folder.name}: profile does not link transcripts.md\")\n+        errors.extend(f\"{folder.name}: broken profile link {link}\" for link in profile_broken)\n+        errors.extend(f\"{folder.name}: broken session link {link}\" for link in inventory_broken)\n+\n+        downloads = folder / \"transcript-downloads\"\n+        for raw in sorted(downloads.rglob(\"*.md\")) if downloads.exists() else []:\n+            exports += 1\n+            if raw.resolve() not in inventory_links:\n+                errors.append(f\"{folder.name}: unlinked full export {raw.relative_to(folder)}\")\n+\n+        sessions = folder / \"sessions\"\n+        for package in sorted(sessions.iterdir()) if sessions.exists() else []:\n+            if not package.is_dir():\n+                continue\n+            packages += 1\n+            for name in (\"transcript.md\", \"summary.md\", \"topic-map.md\"):\n+                file = package / name\n+                if not file.is_file():\n+                    errors.append(f\"{folder.name}: incomplete package {package.name}/{name}\")\n+                elif file.resolve() not in inventory_links:\n+                    errors.append(f\"{folder.name}: unlinked package file {package.name}/{name}\")\n+\n+    print(f\"Checked {len(folders)} student folders, {exports} local Markdown exports, {packages} packages.\")\n+    if errors:\n+        for error in errors:\n+            print(f\"ERROR {error}\")\n+        return 1\n+    print(\"All internal routers and local session links resolve.\")\n+    return 0\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    sys.exit(main())\n*** End Patch";
text(await tools.apply_patch(patch));

Records preserved, excluded from every sweep. Read that folder\'s `CLAUDE.md` before touching anything in it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The primary/mirror boundary (D4 — read before regenerating anything)\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery file in a student folder is one of two kinds, and the distinction decides what a rebuild may overwrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Primary — the brain is the only home.** Transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, study plans, anything Joshua or an agent authored about the student. **Never regenerate, never overwrite; retain forever.**\\r\\n- **Mirror — a projection of a live backend.** Session/booking state (Cal.com + Google Calendar), homework/dossier/publish state (LSAT U Supabase), payment events (Stripe), correspondence indexes (Gmail). On any disagreement the backend wins, silently.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Convention:** mirror files MUST be named `*.generated.md` (or carry `generated: true` frontmatter). A file without that marker is primary by definition and must be treated as precious. Pre-2026-07-16 files (`email-context.md`, `payment-links.md`, `transcripts.md`) predate the marker — treat as primary until individually re-derived and renamed. No Markdown file here is ever the source of operational truth — no package balances, no running totals (all services are paid upfront; Cal.com answers sessions-used).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Standard files per student folder\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the target layout, not a checklist to satisfy with empty files. Create a record when its source or a real teaching judgment supports it. Leave an absent record absent until there is verified content to put in it.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `<student-slug>.md` — internal entry page and folder router (primary). Its `## Open this record` block links only to local files that exist.\\r\\n- `email-context.md` — synthesized Gmail context.\\r\\n- `email-correspondence.md` — Gmail message-id corpus for the student\'s confirmed address.\\r\\n- `payment-links.md` — verified payment, billing, and scheduling links.\\r\\n- `transcripts.md` — transcript and meeting-note inventory. Its `## Local session files` table links directly to every full local Markdown export and every session package, newest first. A Google Doc link is provenance, never the only way to read a session.\\r\\n- `teaching-notes.md` — durable, evidence-backed learning judgments and teaching patterns (primary).\\r\\n- `open-loops.md` — unfinished follow-ups and pending decisions (primary).\\r\\n- `live-status.generated.md` — currently a refreshable LSAT U booking-pass mirror. Calendar, Gmail, and payment state remain in their live systems; this file does not yet summarize them.\\r\\n- `transcript-downloads/` — preserved local exports of Gemini notes (source evidence; do not hand-edit). A raw export here is not itself a completed session package.\\r\\n- `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` — new self-contained session packages.\\r\\n- `assignment-log.md` — running log of assigned work and notification evidence so repeats are avoided (primary).\\r\\n- `Practice_Test_Reports/` — the student\'s PT score reports as received (intake form, screenshots, exports). Raw evidence — read for diagnosis, never edit.\\r\\n- `Student_Specific_Homework/` — work assigned to this student specifically. Distinct from the shared banks in `corpus/Curriculum/*/Homework/`, which are the source material it draws from.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- New student → new folder `students/<slug>/` with a `<slug>.md` profile page.\\r\\n- New session → `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` containing `transcript.md`, `summary.md`, and `topic-map.md`.\\r\\n- Keep the original full Gemini export as a local Markdown file in `transcript-downloads/` when available. Build the session package from verified session evidence, and link both the export and package from `transcripts.md`; older exports may predate the package convention.\\r\\n- `transcript.md` preserves the raw source. `summary.md` records the auto-saved summary, action items, and appended corrections. `topic-map.md` is a timestamped link index to relevant LSAT concepts, student records, and source material.\\r\\n- Session summaries are append-only. Later corrections go in a dated correction section and never erase the original summary.\\r\\n- Generated operational state is clearly marked with `generated: true` and never overrides primary teaching records.\\r\\n- Wikilink student pages into corpus concepts and LSAT U material as they become relevant.\\r\\n- Tier A note: transcripts and correspondence are trackable (Tier-A amendment, 2026-07-16). True secrets (credentials, IDs, payment instruments) still never enter Git — `private/` only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Internal routing contract after each session\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Match the Calendar event and Gemini Doc by guest email, date, and content. Preserve the complete source export in `transcript-downloads/`; never rely on a Drive link as the record.\\r\\n2. Create or update the session\'s `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` package without replacing a primary transcript or summary. Record any source mismatch as a dated correction.\\r\\n3. Add a newest-first row to `transcripts.md` under `## Local session files`: date, direct local full-export link, local package links, and source/status. Include raw-only historical exports and packages even when the other half is absent.\\r\\n4. Update evidence-backed `teaching-notes.md` and `open-loops.md`. Refresh the student\'s `<slug>.md` `## Open this record` block so Joshua, Claude, and Codex can reach the latest work in two clicks. Link only to files that exist.\\r\\n5. Check that all local links resolve, that the full Markdown export contains the transcript rather than only Gemini\'s summary, and that the session index has no unlinked export or package. Leave attendance and missing-source uncertainty explicit.\\r\\n6. Run `python system/scripts/check-student-records.py` from the repo root before calling intake complete. It checks all student routers and every local Markdown export and session package link.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Operating contract\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each new consultation or transcript, Jay 2 must report what was found, what was created or updated, what was inferred, what remains missing, what is recommended, and what Joshua should confirm or reject.    paths.add(path)\\r\\n        if not path.is_file():\\r\\n            broken.append(target)\\r\\n    return paths, broken\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef main() -> int:\\r\\n    errors: list[str] = []\\r\\n ]\\r\\n    exports = packages = 0\\r\\n\\r\\n    for folder in folders:\\r\\n        profile = folder / f\\"{folder.name}.md\\"\\r\\n        inventory = folder / \\"transcripts.md\\"\\r\\n        if errors.append(f\\"{folder.name}: transcripts.md lacks \'Local session files\'\\")\\r\\n            continue\\r\\n\\r\\n        profile_links, profile_broken = linked_files(profile_section, folder)\\r\\n        inventory_links, inventory_broken = linked_files(inventory_section, folder)\\r\\n        if inventory.resolve() not in profile_links:\\r\\n            errors.append(f\\"{folder.name}: profile does not link transcripts.md\\")\\r\\n        errors.extend(f\\"{folder.name}: broken profile link {link}\\" for link in profile_broken)\\r\\n        errors.extend(f\\"{folder.name}: broken session link {link}\\" for link in inventory_broken)\\r\\n\\r\\n        downloads = folder / \\"transcript-downloads\\"\\r\\n        for raw in errors.append(f\\"{folder.name}: unlinked full export {raw.relative_to(folder)}\\")\\r\\n\\r\\n        sessions = folder / \\"sessions\\"\\r\\n        for package in sorted(sessions.iterdir()) if sessions.exists() else []:\\r\\n            if not package.is_dir():\\r\\n                continue\\r\\n            packages += 1\\r\\n            for name in (\\"transcript.md\\", \\"summary.md\\", \\"topic-map.md\\"):\\r\\n                file = package / name\\r\\n                if not file.is_file():\\r\\n                    errors.append(f\\"{folder.name}: incomplete package {package.name}/{name}\\")\\r\\n                elif file.resolve() not in inventory_links:\\r\\n                    errors.append(f\\"{folder.name}: unlinked package file {package.name}/{name}\\")\\r\\n\\r\\n    print(f\\"Checked {len(folders)} student folders, {exports} local Markdown exports, {packages} packages.\\")\\r\\n    if errors:\\r\\n        for error in errors:\\r\\n            print(f\\"ERROR {error}\\")\\r\\n        return 1\\r\\n    print(\\"All internal routers and local session links resolve.\\")\\r\\n    return 0\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nif __name__ == [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"5966cb","wall_time_seconds":1.4051422,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":512,"output":"---\\r\\nname: grilling\\r\\ndescription: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any \'grill\' trigger phrases.\\r\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nInterview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.\\r\\n\\r\\nWork the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven\'t heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user\'s answers before the next round.\\r\\n\\r\\nFormat a round like so:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n❓ **Q1** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\\r\\n\\r\\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n❓ **Q2** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\\r\\n\\r\\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nEach round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. Tune here.\\r\\nQUIET_DAYS = 60\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef quiet_days(last):\\r\\n    \\"\\"\\"Days since a student was last taught. 0 when unknown, so unknown never flags.\\"\\"\\"\\r\\n    import datetime\\r\\n    try:\\r\\n        d = datetime.date.fromisoformat(last)\\r\\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\\r\\n        return 0\\r\\n    return (datetime.date.today() - d).days\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef live_passes():\\r\\n    \\"\\"\\"-> (by_email, by_name, usable, error).\\r\\n\\r\\n    Booking passes straight from the backend. Only `active` and `exhausted`\\r\\n    passes count; `revoked` ones are dropped, because a revoked slug is a dead\\r\\n    link and reporting it as a booking link is exactly the bug this replaced.\\r\\n\\r\\n    Matching to a folder is by email first, then by name, because the two\\r\\n    disagree in practice (Ethan is ethantrie@gmail.com in the backend and\\r\\n    trie@usc.edu in his folder).\\r\\n    \\"\\"\\"\\r\\n    if client is None:\\r\\n        return {}, {}, [], \\"lsatu_api helper unavailable\\"\\r\\n    try:\\r\\n        rows = client().get(\\r\\n            \\"booking_passes\\",\\r\\n            select=\\"booking_slug,student_email,student_name,status,total_credits,used_credits\\",\\r\\n        )\\r\\n    except BackendUnavailable as e:\\r\\n        return {}, {}, [], str(e)\\r\\n\\r\\n    # Matching lives in build_roster_shared so this and build-live-status.py\\r\\n    # cannot drift. I’ve recorded your rule: when a consultation is followed by a student session, the person belongs in an active student folder. When you explicitly say the relationship is over, I’ll remove the folder from the active set and preserve it with former-student records. I won’t infer closure from inactivity.

I checked Calendar: Julian Yoo has only a 2026-09-01 consultation. Venusa has consultations followed by a 2026-09-24 booking titled “Venusa session,” so I created her [internal student folder](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/students/venusa-laveaux/venusa-laveaux.md). That booking does not prove attendance, and I found no transcript for it. The rebuilt [roster](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/students/roster.generated.md) now correctly lists 23 active student folders and excludes former students. The folder link check passes.

**Next grill-me question:** Venusa declined transcription on 2026-09-11, and you agreed to remove it. Should I delete the retained 39-second local export and its Gemini Doc? I recommend deleting both, while keeping a brief record that the source was removed at her request. I have not deleted either.
